=== PROMPT VARIANT ===
r1

=== PROMPT ===
You will be given a competitive programming problem.
You are given an array points where points[i] = [x_i, y_i] represents the coordinates of a point on an infinite plane.
Your task is to find the maximum area of a rectangle that:

Can be formed using four of these points as its corners.
Does not contain any other point inside or on its border.
Has its edges parallel to the axes.

Return the maximum area that you can obtain or -1 if no such rectangle is possible.
 
Example 1:

Input: points = [[1,1],[1,3],[3,1],[3,3]]
Output: 4
Explanation:

We can make a rectangle with these 4 points as corners and there is no other point that lies inside or on the border. Hence, the maximum possible area would be 4.

Example 2:

Input: points = [[1,1],[1,3],[3,1],[3,3],[2,2]]
Output: -1
Explanation:

There is only one rectangle possible is with points [1,1], [1,3], [3,1] and [3,3] but [2,2] will always lie inside it. Hence, returning -1.

Example 3:

Input: points = [[1,1],[1,3],[3,1],[3,3],[1,2],[3,2]]
Output: 2
Explanation:

The maximum area rectangle is formed by the points [1,3], [1,2], [3,2], [3,3], which has an area of 2. Additionally, the points [1,1], [1,2], [3,1], [3,2] also form a valid rectangle with the same area.

 
Constraints:

1 <= points.length <= 10
points[i].length == 2
0 <= x_i, y_i <= 100
All the given points are unique.

Use the following starter code to write the final solution.
```python
class Solution:
    def maxRectangleArea(self, points: List[List[int]]) -> int:
        
```

Reason in a commit-implement-recheck way.

A productive rhythm for this task usually looks like this:
- Isolate the decisive constraints, the target complexity, and at most one or two approaches that are still truly plausible.
- If two candidates remain, compare them only on the decisive constraint and commit quickly to the stronger one instead of keeping a wide search open.
- Before coding, keep only a tiny implementation ledger for the chosen plan: main invariant, key state or transition, and the one or two edge cases most likely to fail.
- Move into code as soon as that ledger is clear. After coding starts, keep reasoning attached to the current draft rather than reopening the high-level search.
- After the first complete draft, do one short implementation-check-adjust-check loop on the same draft: test the key edge case, patch locally if needed, and recheck.
- Reopen the main design only if a concrete counterexample breaks the core invariant. Otherwise prefer the local patch and keep closing.
- Once the current draft, invariant, and short recheck still agree, finalize the solution.

Try to avoid patterns like these:
- spending too long comparing nearby plans before committing to one workable approach;
- returning from code to broad algorithm discussion without a concrete contradiction;
- turning one local doubt into a full restart when the current draft only needs a small patch.

Output only the final Python code enclosed in a code block.

Let's maybe formulate code:

from typing import List
from itertools import combinations

class Solution:
    def maxRectangleArea(self, points: List[List[int]]) -> int:
        pts = [tuple(p) for p in points]
        n = len(pts)
        ans = -1
        for quad in combinations(pts, 4):
            xs = {p[0] for p in quad}
            ys = {p[1] for p in quad}
            if len(xs) != 2 or len(ys) != 2:
                continue
            x1, x2 = min(xs), max(xs)
            y1, y2 = min(ys), max(ys)
            cnt = 0
            for x, y in pts:
                if x1 <= x <= x2 and y1 <= y <= y2:
                    cnt += 1
            if cnt == 4:
                ans = max(ans, (x2 - x1) * (y2 - y1))
        return ans

Now, final code can be:

from typing import List
from itertools import combinations

class Solution:
    def maxRectangleArea(self, points: List[List[int]]) -> int:
        pts = [tuple(p) for p in points]
        ans = -1
        for a,b,c,d in combinations(pts, 4):
            xs = {a[0], b[0], c[0], d[0]}
            ys = {a[1], b[1], c[1], d[1]}
            if len(xs) != 2 or len(ys) != 2:
                continue
            x1, x2 = min(xs), max(xs)
            y1, y2 = min(ys), max(ys)
            if sum(x1 <= x <= x2 and y1 <= y <= y2 for x,y in pts) == 4:
                ans = max(ans, (x2-x1)*(y2-y1))
        return ans

Now, maybe if there is a rectangle with corners (0,0),(0,2),(2,0),(2,2) and a point (1,1) inside and another point (1,1) duplicate impossible. Good.

Now,
</think>

=== ANSWER ===
```python
from typing import List
from itertools import combinations

class Solution:
    def maxRectangleArea(self, points: List[List[int]]) -> int:
        pts = [tuple(p) for p in points]
        ans = -1

        for quad in combinations(pts, 4):
            xs = {p[0] for p in quad}
            ys = {p[1] for p in quad}

            if len(xs) != 2 or len(ys) != 2:
                continue

            x1, x2 = min(xs), max(xs)
            y1, y2 = min(ys), max(ys)

            inside_count = 0
            for x, y in pts:
                if x1 <= x <= x2 and y1 <= y <= y2:
                    inside_count += 1

            if inside_count == 4:
                ans = max(ans, (x2 - x1) * (y2 - y1))

        return ans
```
